景気の底堅さとインフレ圧力の根強さを確認。貿易
赤字はAI関連を含む設備投資需要などを背景に拡
大。非製造業PMIは拡大圏で推移する一方、仕入
価格指数の上昇は企業のコスト負担増を示唆

ユーロ圏総合PMIは節目の50超を維持。小売売上
高も小幅増加。ドイツの鉱工業生産は持ち直したが、
製造業受注は大型案件の反動で減少。景気は緩
やかな回復を維持するも、持続力には不透明感
欧
州
ASEAN経済は国ごとにばらつき。ベトナムは輸出や
鉱工業生産の好調を背景に高成⾧持続。フィリピン
では食料品やエネルギー価格高騰が消費の重石に。
タイでも原油高を背景にインフレ圧力が強まる
APAC
賃金改善が続く一方、個人消費の回復に弱さ。実
質消費支出は前年比減少が継続。消費者マインド

も小幅悪化し、物価高への警戒が根強い。景気動
向指数は低下したものの、基調判断は改善を維持
日
本
先週（10/5～10/12）発表の主な経済指標
米国では金融政策運営の変化と財政懸念、欧州では財政不
安が金利上昇圧力に。欧州では⾧期金利上昇による景気下
押し圧力が、ECBの追加利上げの必要性を低下させる可能性
金利・コスト上昇などの逆風下でも、設備投資意欲は旺盛。家
計の節約志向や企業間の格差は残るものの、価格転嫁・賃上
げも継続しており、日銀の想定するシナリオに沿った動き
注目点② 日銀さくらレポートは企業活動の底堅さを再確認注目点①米欧で金利上昇。異なる背景と金融政策への含意

米国：中間選挙を目前に住宅市場は軟調、アフォーダビリティへの不満が共和党への逆風に
 住宅ローン金利は足元で再び上昇
― 借入コストの負担増により、足元の住宅ローン申請件数は減少
 物価上昇が生活コストを押し上げるなか、金利上昇による住宅取得環境の悪化も家計の不満を助長する要因に
― トランプ大統領の生活費への政策対応は約7割が「期待より悪い」と評価しており、支持率は低下基調
―11月に控える中間選挙では、アフォーダビリティへの対応が引き続き主要な論点

米国：金融政策・財政の両面から米長期金利に上昇圧力
 住宅ローン金利の基準となる米国10年債利回りは9月以降水準を切り上げ、足元（10/6時点）では5.3％近傍に
― 構成要素をみると、追加利上げ観測の高まりを受け、実質予想短期金利が上昇。ウォーシュFRB議長の就任以降、フォ
ワードガイダンスの後退を背景に、経済指標への金利の感応度が高まっている可能性
― 加えて、足元ではタームプレミアムも拡大。中間選挙を控えた家計支援策への思惑などから、財政懸念が意識されてい

る模様。トランプ大統領は10/2、Medicare加入者2,000万人超への90ドルの一時金支給を発表
5

欧州：長期金利上昇を受け、一部ECB高官は追加利上げの必要性低下を指摘
6
 フランスでは国内政治・財政面の不安から長期金利が上昇。イタリア・ギリシャなど周辺の高債務国にも波及
― フランス国債の対独利回り差は急拡大し、一時欧州債務危機（2012年）以来の水準に。その後、大統領選の有力候補と
目されるルペン氏（国民連合）が財政赤字を縮小する意欲を示し、拡大はひとまず一服
―ECBによる介入（国債買い入れ等）の可能性については、複数の高官が検討段階にないとの見方を示す
 長期金利の上昇は需要を抑制。ECB高官からは追加利上げの必要性が低下するとの見方も
― エネルギー高によるインフレ圧力と金利上昇による景気下押し圧力が併存。ECBの政策判断は一段と難しくなる可能性
仏長期金利上昇の経済影響（左）と直近のECB高官発言（右）
（出所）LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 8

7
日本：金利・コスト上昇下でも企業活動は底堅く、設備投資意欲の強さを再確認
 10月のさくらレポートではAI関連需要の拡大とその裾野の広がりを背景に、企業活動の底堅さを確認
― 同一の企業コメントに登場するテーマの組み合わせを集計した共起分析では、「金利×設備投資」の組み合わせが増加。
金利上昇への意識が強まる一方、成長・省人化投資の旺盛なニーズは継続
 賃上げや資産効果が消費を下支えする一方、物価高を背景とした消費の二極化が継続
― 企業は価格転嫁を積極化するも、消費者の節約志向が根強く、商品競争力や顧客層によって転嫁余力に格差
⇒景気・物価は強弱入り混じるも、日銀の想定するシナリオに概ね沿った展開。12月利上げ見通しは不変
企業コメントの共起関係の変化
（注） 同一の箇条書きコメントに、双方のテーマのキーワードがある場合を1件として計上。
なお、テーマ間の因果関係を意味するものではない
（出所） 日本銀行「地域経済報告」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
企業コメント概要
0 10 20 30

金利 × 設備投資
価格転嫁×企業収益
AI関連 × 設備投資
中東情勢 × 価格転嫁
節約志向 × 消費・販売
7月
10月
（件）
設備投資における金利負
担、企業収益における価
格転嫁の重要性を示唆
 AI関連需要は想定を上回るペースで拡大。半導
体製造装置に加え、工作機械や光ファイバー関連
製品など幅広い分野に波及
 金利・建設コストの上昇下でも、競争力維持に必
要な投資を継続。一方、採算悪化や調達コスト増
から投資を縮小・延期する動きも
設備投資・生産
 原材料・人件費の上昇を受け、企業の価格改定は
機動化。値上げ頻度を引き上げる企業がみられる
一方、消費者の価格感応度も高まる
 価格転嫁が進み賃上げ原資を確保できる企業が
ある一方、転嫁不足から利益を削って賃上げを続
ける企業も
価格転嫁・賃金
 株高による資産効果や賃上げを背景に、高額品・
高機能家電・旅行などの需要は堅調
 一方、物価高の長期化を受け、高年層を中心に生
活防衛意識が強まる。低価格品へのシフトや値上

げに伴う販売数量の減少も

世界経済：エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる負担は、所得階層や国によって格差
 生活費高騰に伴うインフレの影響は所得階層により格差
― 食料・エネルギー支出の割合が高い低所得層は負担が大。IMFは低所得層に絞った所得移転が望ましい対応と指摘
 エネルギー・食料の供給ショックによる影響は国家間でも格差
― 先進国では、エネルギー供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果は低所得層で特に大きい
― 食料品への支出割合が高い新興国・低所得途上国では、食料供給ショックによる消費の下押し効果が相対的に大きい

 タイでは過去にも大規模洪水が発生。2011年には、日系自動車企業の部材調達が滞り、日本国内生産に影響した経験も
―2011年の洪水発生時には、工場の被災や物流停滞、人員不足が発生。タイの製造業稼働率は一時30％近く低下
― 当時浸水した工業団地では日系企業約450社が被災。タイ製部品の調達難により、日本でも完成車が一時減産に
 今次局面では、現状、主要拠点への直接被害は確認されておらず、一部工場の生産停止にとどまっている模様
―10月頭時点では各社、大規模な設備被害や減産は見込まず、日本の部材調達への影響も限定的となる見込み
― ただし、10月中旬にかけ更なる大雨が予想されており、操業停止の長期化や被害地域の拡大には依然警戒が必要

日本：個人消費は耐久財特需が剥落。今後は良好な所得環境を背景に緩やかに拡大
12
 8月の消費活動指数は、耐久財特需の剥落などにより、前年比＋0.9％と伸びが鈍化
― 耐久財消費は春先の自動車特需の反動から減少。食品などの非耐久財も、根強い節約志向を背景に弱含みが継続
― 底堅く推移してきたサービス消費も、台風・大雨や猛暑による外出控えの影響などから拡大ペースが鈍化
 今後は良好な所得環境が消費を下支え。個人消費は緩慢なペースながら、底堅く推移する見通し
― 実質所定内給与はプラス圏の伸びを維持しており、基調的な所得環境は良好。増加基調の購買力が消費を下支え
― ただし、インフレ率の再上昇で消費の回復ペースは緩慢。マインドの改善が一服したことも回復ペースを抑える要因に

（注） 旅行収支要因は、非居住者による国内消費（インバウンド消費）を控除し、居住者に
よる海外消費（アウトバウンド消費）を加えるための調整要因。アウトバウンド消費は
SNAの家計最終消費支出に含まれるが、GDPの計算上は輸入として控除される

日本：景気ウォッチャーは消費の緩やかな持ち直しを示唆も、家計・企業とも先行きには慎重
13
 家計動向（現状判断DI）は改善。9月の消費回復を示唆するも、回復ペースは緩慢
― 特にサービス関連の上昇が顕著。8月に増勢が一服したサービス消費は、大型連休や秋の行楽需要を受け持ち直し
― ただし、先行き判断DIは低下しており、個人消費全体でみれば緩やかな回復であることを示唆
 一方、企業動向は、現状・先行きとも慎重。景気回復の勢いは強くない
― 企業動向（現状判断DI）は製造業・非製造業ともに低下。AI関連など需要は堅調も、資材高などの供給制約が重石に

― 先行き判断についても、原材料高や金利上昇、中東情勢の不透明感などが重石となっている模様

日本も「インフレ・金利のある世界」へ。
本稿では下記3つの疑問を解説。
Q1. 足元までの金利上昇は「良い金利上昇」か「悪い金利
上昇」か？
Q2. 先行きの金利上昇の「質」を左右するポイントは何か？
Q3. 日本はどちらに向かうのか、何に注視していく必要がある
か？
問われるのは「良い金利上昇」か
「悪い金利上昇」のどちらに向か
うかで、成⾧戦略、技術革新、
財政規律、市場環境が論点と
なる。

FOMC 成員中有 12 人預計今年將再升息一次 ， 另有4 人認為存在升息兩次的可
能性 ，加上聯準會主席華許(Kevin Warsh)會後表達控制通膨的偏鷹立場 ， 帶動美
元指數走升至 100 高位。
⚫ 歐元區 9 月 ZEW 經濟景氣指數自前月的 31.4 降至 25.8，低於預估的 39.9。數
據下滑顯示在美伊衝突未獲緩解 、 能源價格持續高位情況下 ， 市場對於歐元區經
濟前景抱持較不樂觀的態度。
⚫ 美國公布初請失業金人數為 19.6 萬人，優於市場預期的 20.8 萬，再度呼應聯準
會主席華許(Kevin Warsh)認為目前勞動力市場仍具韌性的說法， 在美國經濟穩
健及通膨頑固的情況下，預計聯準會將持續升息，有利美元持穩。
⚫ 歐洲央行管委暨葡萄牙央行總裁佩雷拉(Alvaro Santos Pereira)表示，歐元區目前

通膨雖未廣泛擴散 ， 惟考量近期天然氣價格飆升 ， 央行將密切關注未來通膨壓力
擴散情形。最新利率期貨市場顯示，歐洲央行今年底仍有再升息一碼空間。
⚫ 日本政府通過消費稅減稅及家庭現金補貼的政策大綱，預計自明年 4 月起的兩
年內，將食品消費稅率自 8%降至 1%，惟大綱中未明確說明資金來源，再度引
發市場對於日本政府的財政擔憂，短線不利日圓表現。
⚫ 台灣央行 9/17 宣布重貼現率連 10 凍維持在 2%不變，央行總裁楊金龍表示，台
灣的通膨相較其他主要經濟體溫和，採取漸進緊縮貨幣政策有助物價穩定。 此
外，目前通膨率為 2%，明年有望降至 2%以下，後續將觀察通膨發展、主要經
濟體貨幣政策緊縮程度及台灣金融情勢，以決定未來貨幣政策走向。

 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Sep/21/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.650，低點在 31.958，以 31.808 作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 1 角 7 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶 ， 美國CPI 數據公布後加強市場對於聯準會 9 月升息的預期 ，
早盤台幣跳空開低，外資連續三日賣超台股並匯出，加以出口商態度轉為惜售，令
台幣接連貶破 31.700、31.800、31.900 關卡， 週四由於聯準會偏鷹派升息，美元指
數走升至 100.300，早盤台幣跳空開低，台幣最低貶至31.958，不過隨後出口商賣壓
出籠，外資雙向進出，台幣收復部分跌幅，維持在31.900 附近震盪；臨近周末，外
資買超台股並於外匯市場匯入 ， 台幣走升至31.722， 最終在央行調節之下收在31.808。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週台幣區間震盪 。 就國際市場而言 ， 美國最新初領失業救濟金人數較預
期減少一萬多人、續領失業救濟金人數亦下降，顯示美國勞動力市場尚屬穩健，加
以聯準會升息抗通膨，鷹派立場給予美元指數支撐、不利台幣表現；就台灣市場來
看，九月台灣央行並未升息，台美利差擴大預計將帶給台幣壓力，此外，儘管聯準
會升息利空出盡，外資買超台股帶動台幣走升，惟台灣即將迎來中秋連假，預計金
融市場交投謹慎，將令台幣陷入橫盤整理格局，綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢區間
震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

以市場靜待聯準會利率決議公布 ， 交投謹慎 ， 人民幣升幅有限 ， 大致維持高檔震盪 ，

6.6944，週線升值 94 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6984，週線升值 21 點。

示，聯準會為明年繼續收緊貨幣政策敞開了大門，美元指數走升，抑制人民幣升值
的速度；就中國國內市場來看，中間價持續偏升將帶動人民幣走升，加以出口商實
質拋匯需求將支撐人民幣表現； 最後就技術面來看 ，USD/CNH 日線圖中 ， 匯價維持
在五日均線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，加以USD/CNH 有效跌破 6.7000 整數關
卡，顯示 USD/CNH 空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣震盪走升。

美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Sep/21/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週日圓走貶。 週初，美國通膨數據維持高位 ，令聯準會升息預期升溫 ，USD/JPY
自 153.29 低點反彈至 155 附近；週間 ，美國聯準會宣布升息一碼 ， 帶動USD/JPY 上
漲至 156.41；臨近週末，日本央行如市場預期升息一碼，惟央行偏鴿派立場令日圓
續貶，帶動 USD/JPY 一度最高來到 158.05，而隨後在日本政府於外匯市場進行匯率
詢價下，USD/JPY 回吐部分漲幅，終場收在 156.86，週線上漲 349 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本政府上周通過消費稅減
稅及家庭現金補貼政策大綱，惟大綱中未明確說明財源，再度引發市場對於日本政
府財政惡化的擔憂，預計將不利日圓走勢。二、日本央行上周五決議將政策利率調
升一碼至 1.25%， 9 名決策委員中有 2 人對升息持反對意見，降低市場對日本央行
進一步升息的預期。另一方面 ， 聯準會上周三亦升息一碼至3.75%-4.00%區間 ，FOMC
政策聲明顯示，18 位官員中有 12 人預計今年將再升息一次，另有 4 人認為存在升
息兩次的可能性，聯準會升息立場明顯較日本央行更為鷹派，預計將支撐USD/JPY
走勢。三、日本財務大臣片山皋月近期再度重申，將於必要時採取外匯干預行動，
以維護外匯市場有序運行。四、就技術面而言，在日線圖中，USD/JPY 短中天期均
線向下發散走勢偏空，惟近期匯價在多次向下測試 154 均拉回，明顯下方有買盤支
撐。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：154.80-159.50

數下滑，令歐元走跌至 1.1525；週間，美國聯準會鷹派升息立場帶動美元走升，歐
元最低來到 1.1460；臨近週末，歐元持續偏弱整理，終場收在 1.1483，週線下跌 107
點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、 歐洲央行管委卡薩克斯
(Martins Kazaks) 表示 ， 為避免美伊戰爭引發的燃料成本上漲開始傳導至工資和其他
商品價格，歐洲央行可能需要漸進式調升利率以抑制通膨。最新利率期貨市場顯示
歐洲央行今年仍有升息一碼的空間，預計將支撐歐元走勢。二、美國聯準會決議將
利率調升一碼至 3.75%-4.00%區間，聯準會主席華許會後偏鷹言論帶動美元指數走
升至 100 上方，預計將限縮歐元走升空間。三、歐元區 9 月 ZEW 經濟景氣指數自
前月的 31.4 降至 25.8，低於預估的 39.9。數據下滑顯示在美伊衝突未獲緩解、能源
價格持續高位情況下，市場對於歐元區經濟前景抱持較不樂觀的態度，將不利歐元
表現。四、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 近期回測年線未能有效突破後明顯
拉回轉為下行走勢，目前匯價承壓於各天期均線下方，空方動能較佔優勢。綜合以
上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。

盤整格局，後續關注下週三財政部公布的第四季公債發行計劃，預估 5 年券
區間為 1.79%~1.83%，10 年券區間為 1.90%~1.95%。

營收走勢圖

研究員/吳大任 TERRY.WU@uni-psg.com

研究部對聯發科的投資建議維持強力買進 ， 目標價5980 元 ， 係因
(1)3Q26 財報大幅優於預期，(2)AI 加速貢獻幅度上修，4Q26 財
測顯著優於市場預期，(3)再度上修 AI 加速器貢獻營收幅度，也
上修市占率，且已有第二客戶，另外 448G SERDES 產品已經 TAPE 
OUT，(4)營收規模擴張速度快 ， 但費用控管好 ，OPM 將開始出現規
模效應，27 年 OPM 以 20%以上為目標。綜合以上，上修聯發科26
年 EPS 預估為 73.12 元(原估 66 元，+11.3%YOY)，並上修 27 年
EPS 預估為 141.16 元(原估 121.05 元，+93.1%YOY)，也上修 28
年 EPS 預估為 298.72 元(原估256.7 元，+112%YOY)。 因為聯發科
的 AI 營收占比推估在 27 年將超過手機，28 年將會成為最大的營
收來源，故以 27 年的 40XPER 或是 28 年的 20XPER，目標價上修
至 5980 元，因為近期盤勢大幅回檔，潛在上漲空間較大，故維持
強力買進評等。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26 財報大幅優於預期： 
聯發科 3Q26 營收為 1681 億元，成長 10.4%QOQ，成長
18.3%YOY，其中主要是 SMART EDGE 成長 20%QOQ 所致(財
測僅給出高個位數增長)，其餘手機及 PMIC 事業體表現約
略符合預期，在法說會中公司表示除了需求強勁外，供應
鏈改善推動也是原因之一。毛利率 46.3%，相對於上一季
提升 0.1 個百分點 ， 與原預估相當 。 雖然營收大規模成長 ，
不過費用控管得宜 ， 使得OPM 攀升至 16.1%(原估 15%)，稅
後淨利達到 282 億元(原估 252.9 億元)，成長 14.6%QOQ，
成長 10.6%YOY，EPS 17.37 元(原估 15.77 元)。

2. 4Q26 AI ASIC 出貨規模上修，超越市場預期： 
公司在法說會表示 ， 雖然3Q26 營收基期較高 ， 加上消費性
電子需求疲軟 ， 不過在AI 加速器量產帶動下 ， 且上修4Q26
貢獻營收程度至 25 億美元(原估 20 億美元)，營收財測給
出 2101 億元至 2218 億元之間 ，1 美元兌 31.7 元新台幣做
計算，成長 25.-32%QOQ，成長 40-48%YOY，毛利率預測為 
45%（±1.5 個百分點） ，營業費用率預測為 26%（±2 個百
分點） ， 並且宣告4Q26 將成為聯發科 OPM 向上轉捩點的開
端。綜合以上，研究部上修 4Q26 營收預估為 2207.3 億元
(原估 2139 億元)，成長 31.3%QOQ，成長 47%YOY，毛利率
上修預估為 44.9%(原估 41.5%)，OPM 同步上修預估為
19.4%(原估 15%)，稅後淨利同步上修為 4090 億元(原估
3208 億元)，成長 46.7%QOQ，成長 78.4%YOY，EPS 25.48
元(原估 EPS 20 元)。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

3. AI 加速器部分只有不斷的好消息，跟市場傳聞相差甚遠： 
A.雲端 CSP 客戶 
聯發科此次法說會上修 AI ASIC 26/27 年營收貢獻，26 年
從 20 億美元上修至 25 億美元，27 年貢獻營收規模達到
160 億美元(原估 120 億美元)，市占率從先前的16-18%上
修至 20%。上修係因聯發科在供應鏈包含材料的供應商協
調順利所致。至於市場更關心的 CSP 客戶下一代加速器，
雖然目前仍碰到良率及載板問題，且公司此次也揭露將會
1Q28 進行量產，因為是CHIPLET 架構，故其中幾顆DIE 已
經完成 TAPE OUT，但較複雜的DIE 將需要較多時間，不過
也按照計畫進行。另外在先進封裝部分，積極與 IDM 及載
板廠商合作，包含日本及台灣供應商，確實有碰到一些困
難，不過距離量產時間還有超過一年，故公司表示仍有信
心準時量產。除此之外，公司也在下一代晶片尋求更多的
產能 ， 且不依賴單一供應商 。 故研究部先前預估28 年聯發
科 AI 加速器貢獻營收達到 320 億美元，研究部目前先暫
時預估不動，未來確認到良率確定上行順利後再進行調
整。 
B.第二個客戶 
公司在法說會上透露， 目前已經有第二個AI 加速器客戶 ，
且與市場上傳聞的中國客戶並非正確，該客戶為非中國廠
商，除此之外，該客戶的產品設計端已經開始，且將在28
年量產 。 研究部認為該客戶為美系客戶 ， 但細節尚未確定 ，
故目前仍未預估入模型當中。 
C.448G SERDES 
目前已經完成 TAPE OUT，持續追趕美系晶片大廠的技術，
雖然在下一代晶片聯發科是提供 336G， 但有望在下下一代
晶片趕上。

4. 獲利將有結構性的提升： 
雖然 AI 加速器的產品毛利率低於公司平均，但因為加速
器單價較高，使營收規模快速放大，而費用可以控制在一
個相對緩步成長的水準，故聯發科認為 4Q26 將會是公司
OPM 向上拐點的開端，27 年的平均 OPM 將會提升至 20%以
上。過去聯發科僅有在疫情時代，產品急速漲價那時候才
有達到 OPM 20%以上的狀況，但後來因為 WFH 結束，庫存
高度堆積，跌損的反噬，使得獲利率大幅回落。本次在AI 
ASIC 帶動，OPM 重回擴張階段，在 AI ASIC 案件有延續性
的前提下，推估聯發科 27-28 年 OPM 將會持續朝 MID 20%
邁進。

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

5. 獲利率優化，持續上修： 
聯發科3Q26受惠SMART EDGE產品需求強勁，加上供應鏈協
調順利，使得財報優於預期，另外在4Q26方面，又上修AI
加速器的貢獻預估，且獲利結構也比研究部預估的更樂
觀，故研究部上修26年聯發科營收預估為6901.2億元(原
估6690億元)，成長15.8%YOY，稅後淨利同步上修預估為
1173億元(原估1059億元)，成長11.3%YOY，EPS 73.07 元
(原估66元)，而27年雖然研究部仍有下修手機及 SMART 
EDGE產線 ， 不過因為AI加速器將貢獻160億美元 ， 故上修27
年 聯 發 科 營 收 預 估1.18 兆元 ( 原估 1.13 兆元 ) ， 成 長
71%YOY，稅後淨利同步上修預估為 2264億元(原估1942億
元)，成長93.1%YOY，EPS 141.06元(原估EPS 121元)。而
28年在第九代產品在 1Q28開始貢獻後，因為該產品使用
2nm製程 ， 加上設計更為複雜 ， 聯發科將參與IP及架構等設
計，單價會大幅提升，加上聯發科OPM結構將持續優化下，
28年聯發科營收預估為 2.21兆元(原估2.17兆元)，成長
87.5%YOY，稅後淨利上修預估為 4794.4億元(原估4117億
元)，成長111.8%YOY，EPS 298.72元(原估256.7元)。

評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%

3Q26 earnings missed: Please see Exhibit 1 .
4Q guidance reflects weak NB demand: Parade is guiding rev. to decline 24% Q/Q
at mid-point, 32% below consensus. We overestimated the stability of demand from
standard-plus customers, while demand from standard customers remains very
weak, as expected. Based on the current demand outlook into 1Q27, it is difficult to
see a V-shaped recovery. That said, we appreciate Parade's supply chain
management and its ability to pass cost pressures on to customers. We see Parade's

TTED business offering L T opportunities amid PCB price hikes.
New business ramp could take time: We previously modelled the company’s ASIC-
like business contributing 15-20% of revenue in 2027-28. Given the complexity of
the chip design and weak DT demand, we believe we were too optimistic. As for the
TTED business, the company expects shipments to exceed 5mn units in 2026, which
could account for ~8% of 2026 revenue. The company expects NB shipments to

remain similar in 2027 vs. 2026, subject to DRAM pricing. Currently, we do not
expect DRAM pricing to decline meaningfully.
Double downgrade to UW; PT NT$444: We cut our earnings forecasts mainly to
reflect weaker 4Q guidance and a lower contribution from the ASIC-like business.
We lowered 2026/27/28e earnings by 14%/39%/43%. The company is now trading at
17x 2027e EPS. We like Parade's long-term growth potential but would need to see a
meaningful resumption in business growth to turn more positive. (continued on the

next page)

3Q26 earnings review: 3Q revenue was NT$4.2bn, -2% Q/Q, -4% Y/Y, and 4% above MSe;

budgets due to allocation to AI spending.

Why it's not too late to downgrade now: The weak 4Q26 outlook lowers the earnings 
base, while visibility on a sustained recovery remains limited. With NB shipments expected 
to remain broadly flat in 2027, TTED growth and initial ASIC-like contributions may be 
insufficient to offset weakness in the core business. The risk of prolonged earnings 
pressure and a slower-than-expected ramp in new businesses supports our more cautious 
view beyond the near-term reset.

Parade: Valuation Methodology
We cut our price target from  NT$818 to NT$444:  Our price target is our base case

bear case scenario values are cut from NT$948 to NT$755, and from NT$418 to NT$288,
respectively.

• 9.8% cost of equity (beta 1.3, risk-free rate 2% and risk premium 6%)

• Terminal growth rate of 4.0%

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

Document 162: 261103_ms_airtac

### Page 1

M
Update
AirTAC International  | Asia Pacific
October Sales -6% MoM/+32%
YoY
Here we focus on AirTAC's monthly sales, which we believe
could be a catalyst for its share price.
What's new:
• October sales came in at NT$3,565mn (-6% MoM/+32% YoY), mainly on 15%
fewer working days, as daily shipments were up 7% MoM.
• By segment, shipments of machine tools (+31% YoY), electronics (+30%
YoY), batteries (+27% YoY), machinery (+25% YoY) and textiles (+25% YoY)

outperformed during the month.
• QTD revenue reached 33% of our estimate at NT$10,918mn (-5% QoQ/+21%
YoY) and 32% of consensus at NT$11,228mn (-2% QoQ/+24% YoY).
Outlook:
• We think 4Q26 revenue is tracking in line with management guidance of
roughly flat QoQ – better than the typical seasonal decline.
• Management remains positive on the growth outlook, driven by share gains
alongside cyclical upturn tailwinds and government policy support.
• We remain OW, as the current valuation of 15x 2027e P/E is undemanding vs.

its mid-cycle average of 25x.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
AirTAC International (1590.TW)
Base case, 29x 2027e P/E. We believe this methodology better captures earnings growth
momentum for industrial automation component plays (market sentiment moves along
with business cycles). As the industry enters an up-cycle, we think applying a near-peak cycle
valuation of 29x P/E is reasonable given the stronger business outlook.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger macroeconomic climate in China

n Stronger-than-expected demand from industrial automation
Risks to Downside
n Sharper economic downturn in China
n Miniature linear guideway business development taking more time
n Larger-than-expected investment in new products but weaker-than-expected demand

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

信邦(3023 TT)  
台灣股市︱電子中游 
投 資 速 報 
買進-首次 目標價 NT$373 (潛在報酬+35.6%) 2026-10-28

半導體設備連接線需求 強勁  
◼ 結 論 與 建 議 
基 於 以 下 理 由 ， 研 究 部 給 予 信 邦 逢 低 買 進 評 等  
1. 1~3 Q26 營 收 達 271.83 億 元 ， 年 增 15.24% ， 3~9 月 營 收 連 增 7 個月 ，
表 現 優 於 市 場 預 期 。  
2. 工 業 （ 半 導 體 設 備 、 自 動 化 ） 、 醫 療 及 車 用 連 接 線 為 三 大 長 期 成 長 引 擎 。  
3. 公 司 半 導 體 設 備 從 線 束 升 級 至 機 櫃 組 裝 大 幅 提 升 附 加 價 值 與 能 見 度；汽 車
業 務 受 惠 電 動 化 與 控 制 模 組 放 量；醫 療 則 受 惠 中 國 市 場 回 溫 可 提 供 近 兩
年 明 確 動 能，人 形 機 器 人 則 為 2028 年 後 的 爆 發 潛 力， 預估 2027 年 EPS  
17.78 元 PE 約 15. 5X， 建 議 買 進 ， 目 標 價 為 373 元 (21* 202 7 年 E P S )。

◼ 重點摘要  
公 司 簡 介  
公司是台灣領先的電子零件整合設計與製造服務商，專注於客製化線束
（ Cable Assembly ） 、 精 密 連 接 器 組 件 及 系 統 整 合 解 決 方 案 。 核 心 業 務 以 提
供從設計、打樣、製造到組裝的完整解決方案。產品以連接線組為主（約
78% ） ， 連 接 器 及 零 組 件 約22% 。 代 理 Hirose 等 國 際 大 廠 連 接 器 。 客 戶 涵 蓋
全球前十大風機廠商中的 8 家、前 十 大 醫 療 成 像 設 備 商 中 的 6 家、前 二 十 大
工業設備商中的 9 家、前十大汽車商中的 6 家等。 2025 年產品應用占比大
致為工業約 31% 、通訊 24% 、 綠 能 20% 、 汽 車 / 航太 16% 、 醫 療 9% （實際
會 隨 年 度 波 動 ） 。 銷 售 地 區 以 中 國 大 陸 為 主（ 約53% ） ， 其 次 為 美 國 、 台 灣 、
歐洲等。積極擴充半導體設備機櫃組裝產能及商用無人機相關產品。  
圖 一 、 1~3Q26 產 品 應 用 佔 比

2

全球半導體設備市場規模快速成長  
根 據 市 調 機 構 數 據 顯 示 ， 全 球 前 段 製 程 設 備 （ Waf er Fa b Equipmen t, 
WFE ）， 2025 年 銷 售 額 創 下 1,169 億 美 元 新 高 ， 預 估 2026 年將再成長
23.1% ，達 1,439 億 美 元 。 此 數 字 較 SEMI 先 前 於 2025 年 底 所 公 布 的 預 測
大 幅 上 修 ， 主 要 受 惠 於 先 進 記 憶 體 、 尤 其 是 HBM 相關 DRAM 技 術 ， 以 及
先 進 邏 輯 應 用 投 資 持 續 增 加 。 隨 著 製 造 商 因 應 AI 應 用 需 求 ， 加 速 擴 充 產 能
及 推 進 技 術 升 級，預 估 2027 年及 2028 年 將 分 別 成 長 21.8% 及 14.1% ，2028
年 市 場 規 模 可 望 突 破 2,000 億 美 元 。  
圖 二 、 全 球 半 導 體 設 備 市 場 預 測

資 料 來 源 ： SEMI 、 康 和 投 顧 整 理 ， 2026/10

工業應用為 最 強 成 長 動 能  
1~3Q26 營 收 占 比 37% ， YOY+38.9% ， 主 要 受 惠 於 全 球 半 導 體 設 備 需 求 擴
張 及 智 能 製 造 自 動 化 升 級 潮 。 內 部 結 構 佔 比 包括 倉 儲 物 流 自 動 化 近 40% 、
半 導 體 設 備 佔 30 ~35% 、 運 輸 / 建設 設 備 線 束 佔 10% 、 能 源 管 理 類 佔 1 0% 。
動能 主 要 來 自 半 導 體 設 備 機 內 / 機 外 訊 號 與 電 源 線 束 組 裝 以 及 倉 儲 物 流 自
動 化 需 求 增 加 ， 27F 營收 YOY+20% 。  
a. 半 導 體 設 備 : 為 最 大 亮 點 ， 從 傳 統 線 束 升 級 至 整 機 機 櫃 組 裝 與 系 統 整 合 。
提 供 機 內 ／ 機 外 訊 號 線 與 電 源 線，並 開 始 整 合 其 他 零 組 件 做 成 小 型 機 櫃
／ 模 組 給 客 戶 。  
✓ 供 應 鏈 地 位 ： 公司 為 全 球 半 導 體 前 段 與 後 段 設 備 大 廠 （ 如  AS ML 、應
用 材 料 等 ） 的 重 要 高 度 客 製 化 線 束 及 系 統 整 合 供 應 商 。  
✓ 應 用 領 域 ： 先 進 節 點 製 造 中 的 光 刻 （ 微 影 ） 、 蝕 刻 和 沉 積 設 備 、 精密
製 程 設 備 中 的 線 束 系 統 、 機 櫃 半 成 品 組 裝 。  
✓ 訂 單 與 產 能 狀 況： 受 產 品 銷 量 增 長 、 複 雜 性 提 升 及 新 客 戶 訂 單 需 求 驅
動，公 司 目 前 通 過 加 班 和 增 加 第 二 班 次 滿 足 產 能 需 求 ；為 進 一 步 擴
充 產 能 ， 斥 資 逾 50 億 元 在 苗 栗 銅 鑼 科 學 園 區 廠 房 擴 建 產 能 ， 主 要
目 的 即 是 用 於 擴 充 半 導 體 設 備 高 階 模 組 與 線 束 生 產 量 能 。 計 劃 於
2Q27 年 正 式 投 入 運 營 ， 預計 增 長 趨 勢 將 持 續 到 2030 年 。

✓ 液 冷 散 熱 技 術 延 伸 : 已 展 示 四 款 數 據 中 心 熱 管 理 解 決 方 案 原 型（ 兩 款 浸

獨 家 訂 單 ， 提 供 整 組 線 束 模 組 （ 獨 家 供 應 ） ， 終 端 應 用 多 在 工 廠 或 倉 儲

美 元 （ 視 整 合 程 度 ， 一 台 可 能 需 要 50 – 150 條 線 束 ） 。  
✓ 技 術 門 檻 高：關 節 線 束 需 通 過 極 高 次 數（ 數 十 萬 至 百 萬 次 ）彎 折 測 試 ，
具 備 耐 彎、耐 壓、防 水、輕 量 化 等 特 性，屬 客 製 化 總 解 決 方 案 （Tota l 
Solution ） ， 多 數 專 案 為 獨 家 供 應 （ Only S our ce ） 。  
✓ 客 戶 ： 已 取 得 約 5 家美系 Tier1 （ 包 括 Figure 等 ） 獨 家 線 束 訂 單 ， 部
分 涵 蓋 中 國 客 戶 （ 以 零 組 件 為 主 ） 。 深 耕 機 器 人 領 域 逾 10 年，從
AGV 、 機 械 手 臂 、 機 器 狗 累 積 經 驗 。  
✓ 策 略 聯 盟 ： 與 宇 隆（ 2233 ）合 作 ， 結 合 線 束 / 控 制 模 組 與 減 速 機（ RV 、
諧 波 、 行 星 ） ， 朝 系 統 級 解 決 方 案 發 展 。  
表 一 、 其他 機 器 人 類 型

資 料 來 源 ： 康 和 投 顧 整 理 ， 2026/10

c. 無 人 機 : 主要 提 供 無 人 機 機 內 線 及 電 池 換 電 櫃 櫃 體 ， 主 要 供 應 2 家 美 系 客

億 元 ， 毛 利 率 約 有 30~40% ， 預 計 於 2028 年 放 量 。

✓ 產品 : 機 內 線 束（ 充 電 線 、 訊 號 傳 輸 線 等 ）、 換 電 櫃（ 電 池 交 換 櫃 體 ），

表 二 、 智 慧 倉 儲 無 人 化 生 態 系 統

醫 療 及 車 用 產 品 穩 定 成 長  
醫療 ： 1~3Q26 營 收 佔 比 9.8% ， YOY+28.4% ， 以 病 人 監 護 與 影 像 診 斷 設 備
內 部 之 傳 輸 線 束 為 主 ( 中系 ) ， GPM> 平 均 ， 27F 營收 YOY+10~15% 。  
a. 產品 : 專 注 於 高 階 大 型 醫 療 設 備 線 束 ， 包 括  CT （ 電 腦 斷 層 ） 、 MRI （ 核 磁
共 振 ） 、 超 音 波 診 斷 設 備  及  加 護 病 房 （ ICU ） 病 人 監 護 系 統 。  
b. 技 術 壁 壘 ： 醫 療 線 束 要 求 極 高 的 生 物 相 容 性 、 抗 干 擾 性 （ EMI ） 與 長 期 高
穩 定 度 ， 認 證 時 間 長 達 2~3 年 以 上 ， 屬 於 「 高 客 製 化 、 高 利 基 」 市 場 。 
c. 成 長 動 能 : 受 惠 中 國 客 戶 加 大 出 口 與 海 外 專 案 進 入 量 產 ， 2026 年 成 長 明
顯 ， 毛 利 率 優 於 平 均 ， 2027 年 預 估 成 長 10 – 15% ， 提 供 穩 定 貢 獻 與 產
品 組 合 優 化 效 果 。  
d. 客戶 : 包括 GE Health car e 、Ph ilips 及 S iemens Healthin eers ， 產 品 滲 透
率 持 續 提 升 。  
車用 :1~3Q26 營 收 占 比 16.5% ， YOY+25.5% ， 充 電 線 （ 電 動 車 與 充 電 樁 ）
及 一 般 汽 車 電 子 訊 號 線（ 胎 壓 偵 測 、 360 度 環 景 、 倒 車 雷 達 等 ） 表 現 持 穩 ，
主 要 成 長 動能 來 自 控 制 器 ， 整 合 5 個 E CU 功 能 （ 含 引 擎 控 制 、 熱 管 理 、 電
池管理、訊號控 制等），提升運算效率。主要客戶為上汽， 27F 營收
YOY+10~15% 。  
a. 產品 : 包 括 充 電 線（ 電 動 車 與 充 電 樁 ）； 一 般 汽 車 電 子 訊 號 線（ 胎 壓 偵 測 、
360 度 環 景 、 倒 車 雷 達 等 ） ； PDCU （ Power Domain Con tr ol Un it ） 控 制
器： PCB 組 裝 產 品 ， 將 過 去 約 5 個 ECU 功 能 （ 引 擎 控 制 、 熱 管 理 、 電 池 管
理 、 訊 號 控 制 等 ） 整 合 成 一 顆 「 大 控 制 器 」 ， 為 26~27 年 主 要 成 長 來 源 。  
b. 主要出貨給中國油電混合車客戶（以上海汽車體系為主，部分品牌有出
口 ） ， 並 擴 展 至 日 韓 等 市 場 。  
c. 展望 : 純 電 動 車 充 電 相 關 今 年 大 致 持 平，成 長 主 要 來 自 PD CU 控 制 器。2027
年 預 估 成 長 10 – 15% 。 長 期 受 惠 電 動 化 與 高 電 壓 ／ 大 電 流 解 決 方 案 需 求 。

評等說明  
1)  強力 買進 / Str on g Bu y ： 我 們 對 該 評 價 個 股 未 來 展 望 正 向 ， 並 預 估 於 未 來 12 個 月 投 資 期 間 ， 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 大 於 50 % 
2)  買進 / Bu y： 我 們 對 該 評 價 個 股 未 來 展 望 正 向 ， 並 預 估 於 未 來 12 個 月 投 資 期 間 ， 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 大 於 25%  
3)  逢低買進 /Tr a din g Bu y ： 我 們 對 該 評 價 個 股 未 來 展 望 正向 ， 並 預 估 於 未 來 12 個 月 投 資 期 間 ， 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 小 於 25%  
4)  未評等 / N ot Ra te d ： 至 目 前 我 們 沒 有 足 夠 基 本 資 料 判 斷 該 公 司 評 等 ； 或 此 期 間 康 和 與 該 公 司 有 特 定 交 易 ， 基 於 規 範 或 康
和 政 策 ， 限 制 予 該 公 司 評 等

免責宣言 :  
本 投 資 報 告 內 容 屬 康 和 投 資 顧 問 股 份 有 限 公 司 版 權 所 有 ， 禁 止 任 何 形 式 之 抄 襲 、 引 用 或 轉 載 。 本 研 究 報 告 所 載 內 容 ， 純 屬
研 究 性 質 ， 所 載 述 意 見 可 隨 時 予 以 更 改 或 撤 回 並 不 另 行 通 知 ， 僅 提 供 康 和 集 團 內 部 同 仁 及 客 戶 參 考 ， 文 中 所 載 資 訊 或 任 何
意 見 ， 不 構 成 ( 且 不 應 解 釋 為 ) 在 任 何 司 法 管 轄 區 內 ， 任 何 買 賣 有 價 證 劵 或 其 他 投 資 標 的 之 要 約 、 招 攬 、 邀 請 、 宣 傳 、 誘 使 ，
或 任 何 不 論 種 類 或 形 式 之 表 示 、 建 議 或 推 薦 買 賣 本 研 究 報 告 所 述 的 任 何 證 劵 。 報 告 內 容 所 提 及 之 各 項 業 務 、 財 務 等 相 關 資
訊 、 意 見 及 預 估 ， 係 取 材 自 據 信 為 可 靠 之 資 料 來 源 ， 既 不 以 明 示 或 默 示 的 方 式 ， 對 資 料 之 準 確 性 、 完 整 性 或 正 確 性 做 出 任
何 陳 述 或 保 證 ， 且 報 告 係 基 於 特 定 日 期 所 做 之 判 斷 ， 有 其 時 效 性 限 制 ， 如 有 變 更 ， 本 公 司 將 不 做 預 告 或 更 新 。 對 於 本 投 資
報 告 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 任 何 證 劵 、 投 資 標 的 ， 或 文 中 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 投 資 策 略 ， 投 資 人 於 決 策 時 應 審 慎 衡 量 本 身 風 險 ， 並 就
投 資 結 果 自 行 負 責 。

研究員/游周翰 Jeffery.Yu@uni-psg.com

研究部維持聯亞買進投資建議，主要考量：(1)3Q26 營收及獲利皆
優於市場預期；(2)AI Data Center 矽光需求持續強勁，主要客戶
訂單能見度超過 6 個月，4Q26 營收可望續增，1Q27 新增產能開出
將帶動營收明顯跳增；(3)管理層維持先前 2027 年產能擴增指引
(+150%YoY)，並且確立2028 年產能擴增速度與 2027 年相當，亦即
兩年擴增產能超過 6 倍。惟考量(1)2026 年新增後段產能驗證時間
較研究部預期長；(2)3Q26 毛利率及費用率優於預期；(3)2027 年
產能增長幅度優於研究部預期 ， 因此調整2026/2027 年營收分別為
55.2 億元(原估 55.92 億)及 155.6 億元(原估 150.4 億)，EPS 分別
為 20.95 元(原估 19.96 元)及 58.76 元(原估 54.06 元)。目標從
3,800 上修至 4,000 元，重申買進投資建議。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26營收、EPS均優於市場預期，矽光出貨帶動獲利成長： 
聯亞3Q26營收15.62億元(+27.9%QoQ，+182.6%YoY)，略低研究
部預期，主因為後段產能驗證時間較研究部預期長 ； 營收雙增
主要還是受惠AI Data Center 矽光產品出貨量持續增加。毛
利率58.6%(+1.1pts QoQ，+15 pts YoY)，營業利益為7.71億
元，營益率達49.4%；營業費用1.43億元(+11%QoQ，+4%YoY)，
主因為研發及員工分紅費用增加；稅後淨利 6.21億元，EPS 
6.12元， 優於研究部預期 ， 主因為毛利率及費用控管優於研究
部預估。產品組合方面，Data Center占營收80–85%，其中以
矽光產品為主 ；Telecom占5–10%， 其他約5%， 與上季比重相當。

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. 4Q26營收持續季增，1Q27新增產能帶動出貨加速： 
展望4Q26，主要客戶目前訂單期間已超過6個月，管理層表示
在手訂單金額已超過2026年全年營收規模， 顯現出目前需求
仍 然 十 分 強 勁 。 管 理 層 預 期 營 收 季 增幅 約 與4Q25 相當
(+16.2%QoQ)，即18.15億元 。 然而 ， 依照過往管理層給予的指
引通常較為保守，且在需求強勁以及新加入的 合作夥伴陸續
開出 後 段 產 能 下 ， 研 究 部 預 估 4Q26 營收為 18.32 億元
(+17.3%QoQ，+185.2%YoY)，EPS為6.77元。

展望1Q27，隨相關設備導入及後段委外產能開始量產，管理層
預期1Q27產出量將有更明顯增幅 。研究部預估 1Q27營收為
275.2億元(+50.2%QoQ，+204%YoY)，EPS為10.26。

3. 美國客戶四年約加持，2028年產出目標為2026年的6.25倍： 
聯亞8M26與美系Data Center 客戶簽訂2027–2030年為期四年
的CW Laser供貨長約 ， 客戶並以保證金方式提供資金支持 ， 以
確保未來產品供應 。 為履行長期供貨承諾 ， 公司同步追加近30
億元資本支出計畫，使過去一年累計核准資本支出超過 80億
元 ， 預計2027年產出顆數較2026年增加150%，2028年再較2027
年增加150%， 相當於2028年產出規模達2026年的6.25倍 ， 其中
8月新增資本支出主要貢獻於2028年。此外，為確保擴產後的
原料供應，公司亦與兩家日系及一家美系InP基板供應商簽訂
長期採購合約 ， 並持續增加台灣及海外後段委外代工夥伴 。 研
究部認為，美系客戶透過長期供貨合約及保證金支持聯亞擴
產 ， 顯示客戶對未來CW Laser需求具有高度確定性 ， 亦有助提
高新增產能的去化能見度，支撐公司2027–2028年營運成長。

4. 多波長CW Laser具技術及ASP優勢，NPO較CPO先行： 
聯亞800G及1.6T矽光產品已進入量產，分別具備8顆雷射及8
至16顆不同波長雷射的解決方案。管理層指出，多波長產品
ASP較單波長高出50%以上，且全球具備大規模量產能力的供
應商有限 ， 有利公司維持產品競爭優勢 。 隨未來光通訊傳輸規
格持續升級，公司亦積極開發3.2T及NPO/CPO應用所需的高功
率CW Laser。其中，管理層預期NPO有望於2H27率先作為過渡
方案，目前所接觸客戶以外部光源設計為主；CPO則因光源配
置 、 連接器等相關規格尚未完全定案 ， 大規模導入時程仍有待
觀察。

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

此外，隨NPO/CPO等應用對光源功率要求提高，未來單顆 CW 
Laser功率提升至250–300mW以上 。 管理層指出 ， 高功率產品因
晶粒面積增加及良率下降，相同InP基板產能可產出的晶粒顆
數可能減少30–50%，使高功率光源對基板及有效產能的需求
增加 。 研究部認為 ， 短期聯亞仍以既有CW Laser產品擴產及多
波長方案出貨成長為主要動能，中長期則隨 NPO/CPO逐步導
入 ， 高功率光源有望成為新增需求來源 ， 並提高相關產品的製
造及供應門檻，將有望成為其技術護城河，維持競爭優勢。

5. Data Center高速PD完成驗證，預計隨新增產能逐步量產： 
除CW Laser外，聯亞亦持續布局Data Center高速PD產品。管
理層指出 ，200G以上PD已完成客戶驗證 ， 預計2027年新增產能
陸續開出，開始量產 Data Center PD 產品。惟目前仍以 CW 
Laser為主要生產重心，因此PD產出規模相對有限，但有助於
降低產品集中度風險。

評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上

3Q26 OP 2.7조, 당사 추정치 35% 상회 
서프라이즈의 근거가 판매보증비 감소에서 발생. 3Q26 기말환율의 낙폭 (-12% qoq)으로 
인한 역설적 비용절감의 규모가 전년동기 대비 1.2조 원을 넘어서며 환율의 조사모삼 효과
로 만들어진 컨센서스 상회 실적. 
긍정적인 부분: 컨틴전시, 신차 효과, 인센티브, 생산 정상화 
지속적인 컨틴전시 체제를 유지하며 비용절감 실천 (=지속된 요소). 4분기 추가적으로 나아
질 요인은 1) 내수 신차효과로 판가 상승 및 유기적 수익성 개선, 2) 공장 가동률 정상화를 
통한 고정비 부담 절감, 3) 해외시장 신차효과를 통한 딜러 인센티브 절감. 
경계할 부분: 재료비, 환율, BEV 판매비중 확대 
재료비 측면에서 1) 2Q26 시작된 미국-이란 전쟁 관련 원재료비 상승은 4Q26F에도 하락
세를 보이지 못할 것이며, 2) 구리, 철강 등의 금속 원자재의 가격 상승 또한 4Q26F 지속될 
것. 또한 3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (mtd 1,338원) 효과로 
환 관련 감익 본격화, IONIQ 3의 출시는 인센티브 절감인 동시에 믹스 관점에서 부정적. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 440,000원으로 소폭 상향 
목표주가 상향 근거는 컨틴전시 체제 유지를 통한 FY27F 자동차사업 부문 이익 예상치 상
향. 부정적 외생변수 (환율, 재료비)가 지속된다는 전제 하에 FY27F OPM 하락을 예상하나, 
(FY26F 5.6%, FY27F 5.2%) 향후 신차효과와 생산 정상화에 따른 자동차 사업부문 수익성 
반등의 여력 존재. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) CES 전후 BD 관련 기대감, 2) 신차 출시 이후 견
조한 판매량, 3) 환율, 원재료비 등 외생변수의 개선.

### Page 1

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 건설/원전 | 2026. 10. 30  
숫자로 증명하는 중

3Q26 Review: 에너빌부문 믹스개선, 두산밥캣 호실적 
두산에너빌리티 3분기 매출액은 4조 4,494억원으로 전년동기대비 14.7% 증가, 컨센
서스에 부합했다. 영업이익은 2,612억원으로 전년동기대비 90.6% 증가, 컨센서스 영
업이익 2,464억원 대비 6.0% 상회하는 호실적을 기록했다. 에너빌리티부문의 원자
력, 가스터빈 매출 증가에 따른 믹스 개선 효과와 두산밥캣의 가격정책 효과에 힘입은 
마진 개선으로 안정적인 실적 기조가 이어지고 있어 긍정적이다. 분기 환평가손실로 
인해 순이익은 여타 수출기업과 유사하게 컨센서스 대비 하회했다. 에너빌부문의 수주
는 누계 10조원으로 목표대비 75%를 채웠으며, 수주 대기중인 안건들을 고려할 때 
당분간 수주 업사이클이 몇년간 지속될 것임이 분명한 시점이다. 
기회가 아닌 경쟁력, 모멘텀이 아닌 수주  
두산에너빌리티에 대해 투자의견 Buy 유지하나, 목표주가를 95,000원으로 하향했다. 
목표 EBITDA는 2035년 EBITDA에 WACC 8%를 적용, 목표 EV/EBITDA는 Global 
Peers인 GE Vernova의 2026년 EV/EBITDA 멀티플에서 자회사 실적 변동성을 고
려해 10% 할인 적용했다. 호실적과 수주 증가에도 GEV의 멀티플 감소와 순차입금 
증가로 인한 NAV 감소를 밸류에이션에 반영한 결과이다. 전일 주가는 한수원의 체코 
원전 시공사 협상 결렬로 인해 7.65% 하락 마감했다. 시공 수주는 아쉽지만 두산에너
빌리티의 체코 원전 기자재 납품에는 전혀 영향이 없으며, 글로벌 전력 수요 증가로 경
제적해자인 대형 가스 터빈 수급 쇼티지는 당분간 지속될 전망임을 고려할 때 전일 주
가는 낙폭 과대로 판단한다. 1) 두산에너빌리티의 가스터빈은 이미 2031년 출하 물량
에 대한 예약 주문이 마무리되고 있으며, 2) 또한 AP1000의 우량한 수주 파이프라인
과, 3) 뉴스케일파워, 테라파워, 롤스로이스, X Energy 등의 SMR 제작을 위한 협업 역
시 지속되고 있다는 점에 주목한다. 전일 기사를 통해 뉴스케일파워 SMR 1호 제작 계
약을 체결했다는 소식을 전했으며, 이제 에너지 트렌지션은 기회가 아닌 회사의 경쟁
력으로, 가스터빈과 원전은 모멘텀이 아니라 수주로 입증되고 있다.  
 Analyst 김세련 
sally.kim@ls-sec.co.kr 
Buy (유지)

2 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Oil Price Dynamics Remain Key to the Outlook
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
Inflation, interest rates and equity market dynamics have changed materially since mid-
August, as widening attacks on Middle East and Russian crude oil and refining capacity
worsened supply and price conditions. Indeed, with the oil market already strained, an
early-September attack on a major Saudi Arabian export pipeline sharply raised concerns

about a protracted and deepening oil crisis. While Middle East crude exports eventually
managed to significantly rebound, increased security risks and freight costs raised
physical crude prices.
Against this backdrop, the spot Brent price surged from $92/barrel in mid-August
back into a $115 to $135/barrel price range until recently. Reflecting unusual market
tightness, other North Sea oil grades were recently trading even higher, up to $160/
barrel, according to the International Energy Agency (IEA). Even as it cooled from $135

to $115/barrel between October 2 and October 20, spot Brent is still almost double
year-ago levels, and so are many diesel prices. The AAA average U.S. retail diesel price
recently hit a new record and was 74% higher year-over-year (YoY) as of October 20.
With insufficient regional supply, the New York Harbor wholesale diesel price is 130%
higher from a year ago, according to the Energy Information Administration (EIA).
Indeed, sharply higher oil prices and a growing diesel shortage have put the global diesel

market under extreme strain. The U.S., for example, has little distillate cushion and
limited spare refining capacity. The distillate inventory (diesel and fuel oil) has decreased
from 37 days of demand coverage in January to just 26 days by early October, according
to the EIA. This 30% drop brought the supply buffer to the bottom of its historical
range. With limited refining capacity and reliance on imports for about 10% of regional
consumption, East Coast distillate inventories are the lowest since 1990, for example,

and are expected to remain below their 5-year average into late 2027, according to EIA
data. More broadly, according to the IEA’s October Oil Market Report (OMR), “oil market
balances are forecast to remain in deficit through the end of the year, with constrained
middle distillate supplies likely to persist well into 2027.”
Thus, although wage growth/labor demand do not appear inflationary, the oil crisis
risks continuing to feed “core” inflation. According to the Federal Reserve’s Beige Book 1

covering precisely the mid-August to early-October period of heightened oil-market
turmoil, “higher oil prices permeated all District reports,” with firms noting that fuel
surcharges were passing on increased costs throughout the supply chain. The 22% rise
in airfares over the past year alone contributed around 0.2 percentage point (pp) to the
2.6% 12-month increase in the “core” consumer price index (CPI) for September. Aside
from transportation costs effects, the spike in diesel prices also portends high food

prices with a few-month lag given planting/harvesting cost considerations.
By solidifying expectations for higher Fed interest rates to fight inflation, the surge in oil
prices has contributed to the spike in long-term Treasury yields this year. According to an
October 13, 2026, Empirical Research Partners report, the Iran-war oil crisis is estimated
to have contributed about 0.50 pp to the roughly 2.40 pp increase in the real U.S. long-
term Treasury rate from 2020 to early October (Exhibit 1A). According to the report, this

oil-price effect accounts for “much of the rise in real rates since February 27, 2026...”
On the positive side, while most other underpinnings for higher rates compared to the
pre-pandemic decade are structural (Exhibit 1A), the oil-price-related effect on interest
rates is reversible, implying that a resolution to the current oil crisis has the potential to
help lower both inflation and interest rates, relieving some of the current headwinds to
growth and asset prices.

Indeed, while much of the increase in interest rates since the pandemic reflects stronger,
more normal economic growth, and has thus been accompanied by surging corporate
1 Summary of Commentary on Current Economic Conditions by Federal Reserve District, Federal Reserve, October 2026.
Investment Implications
Oil market conditions remain a swing
factor for the outlook. A shift lower in
oil risk premia and improvement on
the refining front would go a long way

toward easing inflation and interest
rates while bolstering risk-asset
appetite. A gradual increase in Fixed
Income portfolio duration continues to
make sense, in our view.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
earnings and risk-asset prices, worsening oil-market conditions since mid-summer and
associated spike in yields have been accompanied by a loss of momentum in U.S. equity
prices (Exhibit 1B). Given their higher sensitivity to energy price and interest rates shocks,
Small-caps lost relatively more momentum than Large-caps during this period.
The Large-cap rally also faded, though, as oil and interest rate headwinds widened stock-

level return dispersion, with roughly 40% of constituent stocks in bear market territory
(down 20% or more). Still, buoyed by the Technology sector as well as by expectations
of conflict resolution, the market-capitalization weighted S&P 500 Index has mainly
consolidated just below its peak. While the equal-weighted S&P 500 Index stumbled more,
it was just about 3% off its peak as of October 20.
While the economy remains resilient and profit expectations remain strong, persistent

oil market turmoil makes the macro backdrop more fragile. For example, the Beige Book
not only highlights concern about spreading fuel-related inflation, as noted above, but its
language also suggests that economic activity lost some momentum since its mid-July
to late-August report. Overall, it describes growth as slight to moderate, with generally
moderate manufacturing growth except for robust activity in the energy-heavy Dallas
District. Little growth is reported in other sectors overall, with most Districts seeing little

to no change in consumer spending, for example.
Employment was described as stable to little changed, with generally adequate labor supply
except for certain specialized trade workers. Five of twelve Districts note some reports of
businesses cutting staff through attrition, delaying hiring or anticipating less job creation
as a result of AI-driven productivity gains. Most Districts reported that business maintained
a positive outlook for coming months, though “expectations tend to be for slight growth,

weighted toward manufacturing and burdened by persistent uncertainty.”
The September employment and retail sales reports seem to validate the anecdotal Beige
Book evidence. Also, the 0.4% annualized manufacturing production growth over the July to
September period (and 1.1% YoY, according to the Federal Reserve Broad), while welcome,
is hardly an indication of booming conditions. Against a backdrop of rising electricity prices,
wage growth lagging inflation, and higher gasoline prices broadly offsetting this year’s tax

cut benefits, real consumer spending is unlikely to accelerate much absent stronger labor
demand and real wage growth. A resolution to the oil crisis that brings economic relief and
tempers interest rates is thus critical for continued expansion in 2027, in our view.

Investment Implications
Portfolio maintenance is a key
component of successful investing.
It’s time for investors to assess their
portfolios—or look under the hood.
MARKET VIEW
Six Questions Investors Should Be Asking Themselves Right Now
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
After four years of rising equity prices, investors have plenty to celebrate. Despite

inflation scares, geopolitical tensions, trade wars, rising interest rates and breathless
predictions around AI, the S&P 500 has returned some 131% (total returns) since the
bull market in equities commenced in October 2022. Corporate earnings hav e generally
exceeded expectations; AI has ignited a historic investment boom, and America’s
technology giants have powered the market to new heights.
We believe the bull will rumble on into 2027. We also believe that now is the time for

investors to take stock of their portfolios, since bull markets have a way of changing the
composition of portfolios. In addition, investors should not assume that the investment
strategies that worked well in the past four years will necessarily deliver comparable
results during the next four. Below are six questions investors should be asking
themselves right now:
Number One: How concentrated is my portfolio? Many investors believe they are
invested in the stock market when, in fact, they are invested in a handful of companies—

specifically AI-related names that have soared and dominated the index over the past
few years. Information technology now accounts for roughly 40% of the market cap of
the S&P 500, while the top 10 companies represent a share of 38.4%.
As the Financial Times recently noted, U.S. equity and credit markets have become so
increasingly concentrated in AI-related groups that “more than tw o-thirds of companies
in the Russell 1000 index of large U.S. groups are linked to AI, either directly or

indirectly or because the technology is becoming embedded in their business models.” 2
Meanwhile, Big Technology hyperscalers and companies that are beneficiaries of the AI
spending boom make up about 16% of the U.S. high-grade bond market after a huge
borrowing binge, making the grouping the largest in the investment-grade bond market. 2
The upshot: Investors that have been rewarded by concentration need to ask themselves
what percentage of their wealth is tied to the all-encompassing AI trade and consider

what would happen if mega-cap tech leadership falters.
Number Two: How diversified is my portfolio? While concentration asks whether
investors own too much of a few assets, diversification asks whether investors own enough
of different assets that behave differently when economic cycles or financial conditions
change. A portfolio containing hundreds of U.S. Equities via exchange-traded funds, mutual
funds and other assets may appear diversified. But if those companies are overwhelmingly

exposed to the same economic cycle, interest rate environment, or technology theme,
the diversification benefits are diluted. Case in point: While we remain constructive on
Emerging Market (EM) Equities, investors need to understand that a significant driver of
EM returns has come from AI-driven markets like Taiwan and South Korea.
Today, true diversification means considering exposure to hard assets (commodities, real
estate), EM mining and agricultural leaders, EM bonds and currencies, China bio-technology,

Small-cap international, and Private Equity and Hedge Funds for qualified investors.
Number Three: How tax-efficient is my portfolio? Bull markets create wealth—in
addition to tax liabilities. The good news is that after four years of rising equity returns,
investors are sitting on sizable, unrealized capital gains. The bad news: Market gains
can feel like a financial straitjacket, making it difficult to rebalance portfolios without
triggering significant tax liabilities.

Against this backdrop, now is the time for investors to review where the assets are
held—distinguishing between taxable and tax-advantaged accounts, and the attendant tax
ramifications. The appropriate mix depends on individual circumstances, including future
2 “Investors seek shelter from heightened exposure to AI”, The Financial Times, October 6, 2026.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
withdrawal taxes. It is also time to consider tax-loss strategies, charitable donations of
securities and other tactics and strategies since in the end, the measure of inv estment
success isn’t simply how much money investors make. It’s how much they keep.
Number Four: Is it time to take profits?* There is a peculiarity to bull markets: The
more money investors make, the more the attendant tax liabilities. Tax liabilities, in turn,

can discourage selling, and the reluctance to sell can leave investors dangerously exposed
to yesterday’s leaders and compound concentration risks. That brings up an emotionally
difficult decision: When should investors sell their leaders or take some chips off the table?
The question is notably relevant today, given four years of substantial Equity gains.
Beyond tax considerations, investors need to understand that profit-taking can create
financial flexibility. Realized after-tax gains can replenish cash reserves, fund near-

term spending needs, reduce leverage or most importantly, provide capital to rebalance
portfolios in other areas of the market. In general, the goal of taking profits isn’t to get
out of the market—it is to ensure that after years of accumulated gains, these proceeds
are redeployed and redistributed across various asset classes that continue to support
and underpin long-term wealth accumulation.
Number Five: How well is my portfolio positioned for a new interest rate

regime? For much of the past quarter century, investors operated in a world of copious
liquidity, declining interest rates and relatively cheap capital. The era of capital abundance
favored stocks over bonds, but that era is over—we are shifting to a world of capital
scarcity, or intense competition for capital and the attendant hike in the cost of capital.
In this world, higher rates weigh the most on long-duration assets. Positioning for this
regime favors companies with strong balance sheets and cash flow over firms with heavy

debt or refinancing needs. High-quality bonds are more attractiv e in this environment;
sovereign debt risks are concentrated more overseas (i.e., France) than the U.S.
Number Six: How well is my portfolio positioned for the next shock? Shocks
happen. If the past four years have taught investors anything, it is that the unexpected
should be the expected. This decade alone has produced gut-wrenching market mo ves
related to a global pandemic, a ground war in Europe, the reconfiguration of global

supply chains, epic technological change and a war in the Middle East. Through it all, U.S.
Equities have trended higher but hardly in a linear fashion.
The next shocks could emerge in the global bond market, where mounting go vernment
debt and higher borrowing costs have triggered mounting volatility. It could emerge from
disappointing returns on massive AI investments. Or it could come from a geopolitical
event, another energy shock or any other unforeseen event. Investors can’t predict the

next shock, but they can prepare for it. So now is the time for investors to stress test
their portfolios against a potential shock, examining how a steep downdraft in Equities
or spike in bond yields would affect their retirement plans, liquidity needs and other
financial goals and objectives.
Exhibit 2: Six Questions to Stress Test Your Portfolio.
The six questions What investors should examine Portfolio diagnostic
1. How concentrated am I? Mega-cap technology exposure and overlapping holdings % of portfolio in top 10 stocks

2. Am I truly diversified? Asset allocation, geographic mix and correlated exposures Current equity/bond mix versus target
3. How tax-efficient am I? Unrealized gains, asset location and tax-loss harvesting Embedded capital gains and estimated tax cost of rebalancing
4. Is it time to take profits? Portfolio drift, oversized winners and valuations Positions exceeding target allocation
5. Am I ready for higher interest rates? Duration, leverage, refinancing risks and cash flows Bond duration and exposure to highly leveraged companies

6. Am I prepared for the next shock? Liquidity, downside exposure and portfolio resilience Estimated loss in a 20% equity selloff

* “Pop the Champagne,” Portfolio Insights, Chief Investment Office, August 2026.

6 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are rising credit spreads signaling about the broader market environment?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
Rising French bond yields with a sharp rise in spreads above German bunds, mixed
with AI buildout concerns, are creating nervousness across the capital markets. This
nervousness centers around the ability to fund deficits, the potential for slower growth

and the future return on the announced capital investment. We expect these concerns
to continue to linger well into next year as growth worries remain in France, bond
yields stay sticky around current levels, and, in the case of AI, investors demand more
compensation for financing.
At this stage, we do not expect the rising spreads in targeted areas to be a precursor
for a material slowdown in economic growth overall. Rather, we believe this is a natural

signal that investors are moving up the quality stack as we move into the middle of
the AI buildout. The bottom line is that rising credit spreads in lower quality corners of
the market are natural along the lifecycle of financing. When free cash flow is called
into question, or when debt financing costs begin to approach the expected return on
investment, investors begin to reprice risk.
We continue to emphasize higher quality balance sheets in corporate and sovereign

credit as well as higher quality leaders in the AI buildout overall. The closer we get to
2027, investors will likely begin to transition from too much exposure to the “buildout”
and not enough exposure to “AI adopters.”

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
SS&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and
services.
S&P 500 Market-cap Weighted Index is a float-adjusted market-capitalization-weighted index, meaning a company's influence and percentage weight in the index are determined by its
total market value (share price multiplied by the number of outstanding shares).

S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is a variation of the traditional S&P 500 where every single company in the index is given the exact same weight—0.2%—regardless of its size or market
capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Industrials Index imposes equal weights on the index constituents included in the S&P 500 that are classified in the GICS® industrials sector.
Russell 1000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of the 1,000 largest publicly traded companies in the United States.

Bank of America, Merrill, their affiliates and advisors do not provide legal, tax or accounting advice. Clients should consult their legal and/or tax advisors before making any financial decisions.
This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase
or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

Document 330: Europe’s Deal Limiting Chinese Auto Imports (2026-11-02)

Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-11-02

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
Europe’s Deal Limiting Chinese Auto Imports
Europe’s auto deal with China is a good start. But containing China’s export boom
will require a much broader response.
European Commissioner for Trade and Economic Security Maros Sefcovic speaks at a press briefing at the EU Delegation to
China in Beijing, China October 9, 2026. Florence Lo/Reuters
PUBLISHED
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
November 2, 2026 11:02 a.m.
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
The year-over-year increase in Chinese automobile
exports to all markets is currently about 3.5 million
cars.
Over ten years, that is 35 million cars. The global
market excluding China is only around 60 million
cars. Japan raised its auto exports by about 3.5 million
cars in the 1970s—but that was over a decade, not
one year. And right now the U.S. and Indian markets

(around 20 million cars) are effectively closed to
Chinese production. The 2026 jump in exports is an
incredible.
China’s domestic market, though, is down 20 percent
this year—that works out to a decline of about 5
million cars. The Wall Street Journal estimates that
domestic sales will fall to around 19 million cars this
year, less than a third of the production capacity of
China’s auto industry.
So even though China’s exports are increasing at an

unprecedented pace, China’s domestic auto sector
isn’t really booming; production is essentially flat, and
intense domestic competition is destroying the
profitability of firms that don’t export.*

### Page 3

3
Europe’s new auto deal with China should be
understood in this context.
The deal limits China’s exports of hybrids (including
plug-in hybrids) to Europe to around 350,000 cars.
That is above the 300,000 hybrids that China
exported to Europe in 2025, but well below the (gulp)
800,000 annualized pace for hybrids in the last three
months of (European) data. Total imports,
annualized, were running at an incredible 2 million a
year pace thanks to strong battery electric imports in

September.

### Page 4

4
China appears to have concluded that it prefers a
guaranteed market for a limited number of cars (and
more or less guaranteed profits) to the risk of a
prohibitive tariff that would cut off all its exports.
And Europe preferred a negotiated deal to the risk of
retaliation and a slowdown in licensing rare earth and
magnet exports.
The Commission also thinks of this as a bit of a
model—more on that later.
I certainly understand the logic of the deal.

Something needed to be done. China’s export of
hybrids to Europe were up an insane 200 percent
year-over-year in the last 3 months of data, which led
to a 75 percent increase for all cars. One more year of
75 percent growth would bring China’s exports to

### Page 5

5
around 3 million cars; in two years, close to 6 million,
and in three years, 10 million—or most of the market.
But Europe still is a long way from a coherent trade
and industrial policy with respect to autos, let alone a
coherent economic policy towards overall trade with
China.
After this deal, as I understand it:
Hybrid imports are capped at 350,000, down
from a projected 800,000 in 2026—and then
rising to 370,000 and 390,000 in subsequent
years.

Battery electric vehicles face a tariff from 25
percent (Telsa, 10 percent base + 15 percent) to
45 percent (10 percent base plus 35 percent).
BYD’s total tariff is at 27 percent, which isn’t
prohibitive. Even with these tariffs, battery
electric imports will total around 600,000 this
year, up from 500,000 last year.
Diesel and gasoline (ICE) cars face a tariff of
10 percent. Europe is importing around
200,000 of these a year (including a significant

number from Chinese-owned MG).
There are no special tariffs on imported parts or
imported batteries, which matters for the growing
number of Chinese factories in Europe. So long as
tariffs are low and the renminbi is undervalued,
Chinese firms still have an incentive to maximize the

### Page 6

6
Chinese content of their “transplant” factories (ask
Thailand).
There is currently no unified European policy on
inward investment that requires joint ventures or
technology transfer either, only a lot of wishful
thinking.**
Sum it up, and it is a market where Chinese exports
are constrained by a hodge-podge of different trade
defense tools, and there is no standard policy for
ensuring that Chinese assembly operations use
European parts rather than import Chinese kits. The

existing operations, which are just starting to scale up,
don’t draw on a European supply chain. They likely
preserve a few assembly jobs at the expense of the
broader automotive ecosystem.
A coherent European (or national) industrial policy
for the automotive and battery sectors is thus still a
work in progress.
The safeguard on hybrids and the resulting tariff rate
quota only stops the bleeding.
And make no mistake, Europe’s position in the global

automotive sector is slipping. Europe still runs an
overall surplus in auto trade, but that surplus is falling
fast if trade in batteries is added to automotive trade
(there isn’t a split between batteries used for energy

### Page 7

7
storage and batteries used by cars in the EU data
right now, at least not that I have found).
Europe’s broader trade policy to China is similarly a
work in progress.
The surge in imports of hybrid cars was particularly
pronounced, but it is by no means the only such surge.
The balance in industrial machinery has eroded
significantly. Consider the following chart, which
shows imports and exports of machinery, electronics
and vehicles (HS 84, 85, and 87) by weight.

### Page 8

8
The balance in chemicals, measured by weight to
abstract from price changes, shows the same trend.
Europe doesn’t have an across-the-board tool for
applying tariffs against China like the U.S. section
301 statute.

### Page 9

9
But that isn’t necessarily an immediate constraint, as
there are plenty of solid safeguard cases thanks to the
current surge in European imports from China.
But if each safeguard needs to include a negotiated
tariff-rate quota that gives China enough guaranteed
market access (and Chinese firms enough guaranteed
profits) to ensure trade peace, Europe needs to get
moving with negotiation in other sectors.
Chemicals and machinery are more diverse sectors

than cars and thus aren’t as amenable to the same
high-level Š ef č ovi č -Wang negotiations the led to the
most recent deal.
This critique isn’t meant as criticism. Europe needed
to act on cars. Look at a chart of automotive trade in
kilos.

### Page 10

10
It’s just that it also needs to act in other sectors: auto
parts, batteries (though that is complicated),
chemicals, and some categories of industrial
machinery. The scale of China’s export boom and the
fact that in most sectors there are no limits to China’s
ability to scale up is a real problem.***
And Europe cannot wait for China to conclude that
it would rather grow on the basis of internal, rather
than external, demand.
I disagree with the IMF’s former top brass on this. A

China that grows on the back of a 1.5 pp net export
contribution for another couple of years will
deindustrialize much of the world. China’s trading
partners cannot step aside and do nothing until
China figures out how to escape deflation using its
own demand. Xi appears more than happy to
continue with his current policy mix and gamble that

### Page 11

11
the world will continue to absorb China’s ever
growing export surplus.
Pascal Lamy, Nicolas Köhler-Suzuki, and Victor Luiz
do Prado of the Jacques Delors Institute recognize
the need for a more systemic response—as do Jean-
Pierre Landau and Sébastien Jean, who have done an
excellent job explaining how China’s micro- and
macro-industrial policies fit together into a coherent,
self-reinforcing whole.
The proposals in the Lamy et al. paper overlap heavily

with the Commission’s current approach to China.
The authors envision a two-pronged approach: first,
negotiating with China to seek agreement on tariff-
rate quotas that effectively limit the EU’s imports
from China; and second, developing a broad yet
targeted tariff-based instrument to address structural
imbalances should negotiations fail. The latter would
give Europe a credible threat should China refuse to
agree to the proposed safeguards.
My own view: don’t let the perfect be the enemy of

the good.
The EU has shied away from developing its own 301
tool out of concern about preserving a “rules-based
system.” I still think a European 301 would allow a
more fulsome response, but understand that
European isn’t as willing as the United States to use

### Page 12

12
tools that at least appear to be outside those
authorized by the WTO. In reality the EU no longer
really wants to trade with China on the same (MFN)
terms applied to other WTO members. That mirrors
the current view of the United States. But the EU is
willing to go through more procedural hoops to get to
a similar result.****
But I also recognize that, absent policy adjustment in
China—notably a stronger currency that allows for
fair market competition—the scope of safeguards and

managed trade will progressively expand.
* One sign: Stellantis is resuming Jeep production in
China, but primarily for export rather than the
domestic market.
** There is a lot of (in my view) somewhat confused
analysis of Chinese investment that works off the
analogy provided by either foreign investment in
China’s auto sector or Japanese investment in the U.S.
and Europe in the 1980s. These are relevant examples
of course, but asserting that Chinese investment will

result in a similar outcome when the underlying
economics differ is a big logical jump. Japanese firms
had incentives to localize their parts production in the
United States from the then-strong yen as well as
from the risk of protection. Foreign firms operating in
China had to do JVs until recently and had incentives
to localize from China’s significant tariff on auto

### Page 13

13
parts and China’s lower cost structure (in part because
of a weak RMB). Tesla’s Shanghai incentives and tax
deal included strong localization incentives, and there
were additional incentives from Tesla’s desire to
qualify for the lists the governed access to China’s
consumer EV subsidies. That is why something like
90 percent of Tesla parts, including its batteries, are
now made in China. Chinese investors in Europe
have no similar incentives to localize right now, yet

many assume that the results will be the same. That,
to me, is naïve. Since the underlying economics differ
(Europe’s cost basis is much higher than existing
production in China) getting Chinese firms to do
more than final assembly in Europe will take stronger
policy tools than were required to get foreign firms to
localize parts production in China. Europe’s
Industrial Accelerator Act would be a step in the
right direction, but more is likely needed. Thailand

provides a useful cautionary example.
*** China can make over 60 million cars, according to
recent reporting from the Wall Street Journal. The
domestic market this year will be under 20 million
cars (down from something like 24 million cars last
year). Even with exports of 10 million, there is ~30
million cars worth of unused auto production
capacity (roughly double European demand). And
China is still investing at a pace that is consistent
with adding close to 5 million of EV production

capacity a year. Chinese firms, who don’t make any
money on domestic sales, are strongly incentivized to

export. The world has never encountered such elastic

**** The more different the legal basis for the
European and U.S. response—and the more policy

harmonized policy isn’t even a U.S. goal.

Document 353: Inside the cost-of-living squeeze Households are weathering higher prices, but housing costs remain a key strain. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Inside the cost-of-living squeeze Households are weathering higher prices, but housing costs remain a key strain. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-11-01
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

13039171     1

Economy 
Inside the cost-of-living squeeze 
04 November 2026

Key takeaways 
• Not every essential has become less affordable. Despite the cumulative rise in prices, households are spending a slightly smaller 
share of income on gas and groceries than they did several years ago. That suggests the affordability squeeze is less broad-
based than the inflation headlines alone might imply. 
• Housing is the major exception, and the pressure extends beyond lower-income households. Both mortgages and rent have 
increased since 2019. Some lower-income renters look particularly stretched, but around one in four higher-income mortgage 
holders also spend more than half their income on housing. 
• Wage gains and changing behavior have helped households absorb higher prices. But real wage growth has mostly softened, 
which may help explain why sentiment remains weak even as consumer spending continues to grow. In fact, declining sentiment 
likely reflects the sacrifices consumers have to make in order to keep spending.

Everyday essentials take up less income than they did four years ago 
Higher prices are still top of mind for consumers, especially when it comes to affordability. But just how stretched are 
households? Using Bank of America internal data, we look at how much of their income households are spending on everyday 
essentials like gas, groceries, mortgages and rent, and how that has changed over the past four years across income groups. We 
also explore how wage growth and shifts in spending habits have helped households cope with higher prices, and why consumers 
still feel downbeat even as they keep spending. 
For example, while consumers overall spent slightly more of their income on gas and groceries compared to last year, outlays for 
these necessities are still below what they were four years ago, according to Bank of America payment and deposit data (Exhibit 
1). Combined, gas and groceries accounted for nearly 13.5% of income in 2026, 10 basis points more than last year, but almost 
half a percentage point less than four years ago. And even with price increases over the past year, most consumers only spent 
3.2% of their income on gasoline, up from 2.9% a year ago and below the 3.4% from four years ago.  
Exhibit 1: Consumers overall spent slightly more of their income on gas and groceries compared to last year, though spending on these necessities 
is still below pre-pandemic levels

Source: Bank of America internal data 
Note: 2026 data is through September.

Across income groups, each cohort follows a similar pattern, though lower-income households do allocate the highest share of 
their income to gas and groceries (Exhibit 2).  
Exhibit 2: All income cohorts have experienced an increase in gas spending as a share of income, offsetting a decrease in grocery spending in 2026 
Median gas and grocery spending as a share of income by household income tercile (annual, %)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Housing stress isn’t just a low-income story 
Housing is an altogether different picture. Whether you’re a homeowner or a renter, housing is often the biggest expense of any 
consumer, accounting for around 30% of income. Median mortgage payments have steadily risen over the past two years and 
now account for more than 30% of a homeowner’s income, according to Bank of America internal data (Exhibit 3). 
While renters have experienced some easing, they tend to set aside more of their income to housing costs (Exhibit 4). Rents as a 
share of income peaked in 2025, with renters spending more than 32% of their income on shelter. Even so, a small improvement 
helps. Due to declines in housing payments, renters have seen a significant improvement in discretionary spending over the past 
year (read more in On the move: Renovation over relocation?). Also, some housing related costs have also eased in the past year 
(read more in Energy bills: Short-term relief, longer-term challenges and Insurance costs hit the brakes).  
Exhibit 3: Mortgage payments as a share of income have risen over 
the past two years…

households (Exhibit 5). The converse is true for renters, with lower-income families spending around 35% of their paychecks on 
rent, five percentage points more than higher earners (Exhibit 6).

Some financially stretched households may be particularly affected by rising housing costs 
Notably, in the first nine months of the year, roughly one in four lower-income renters spent more than half of their income on 
rent, up from about one in five in 2019 (Exhibit 7). One positive note, the share of renters who spend most of their income on 
rent does seem to have declined slightly in 2026.  
Financial pressure from housing is not limited to lower-income households. Around one in four higher-income mortgage holders 
spend more than half of their income on home payments (Exhibit 8). And while only slightly more than one in five lower-income 
mortgage holders spent half of their income on housing, this is a nearly five percentage point increase compared to pre-
pandemic levels.

Wage growth and changing habits are insulating some households from inflation 
Still, some families have not seen their bills, as a share of income, rise alongside inflation. Why is this? In our view, this is likely 
because after-tax wages have increased significantly for these people. This is especially true for some lower-income households 
that have landed steady employment since the pre-pandemic era, according to Bank of America internal data and the Consumer 
Price Index (CPI) (Exhibit 9).  
Additionally, consumers have adjusted their behavior in response to higher prices. For one, we may be driving less than before 
the pandemic. Despite some increases in vehicle miles driven per capita over the past four years, we’re still getting behind the 
wheel less on average compared to 2019 levels, according to the US Census Bureau and Bureau of Transportation (Exhibit 10). 
At the same time, some households have “traded down” to less expensive stores or items and services (read more in The rise of 
value).  
Exhibit 9: Some lower-income households have seen their wages 
grow faster than inflation since 2019 
After-tax wages, based on Bank of America deposit data (three-month 
moving average, index 2019 = 100) compared to CPI inflation, based on 
Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS) data (monthly, index 2019 = 100)

Source: Bank of America internal data, BLS 
Note: Data not available for October 2025 due to federal government shutdown. 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

pandemic levels 
Total vehicle miles traveled per total resident and civilian population 
estimates (monthly, index 2017 average = 100)

Source: US Census Bureau, Bureau of Transportation   
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Inflation is driving households to make more sacrifices 
There is one caveat to the affordability picture: wage growth among lower-income households slowed significantly throughout 
2025, though they’ve recovered since then. Furthermore, some middle- and higher-income families have not seen their pay keep 
up with inflation, at least compared to 2019 levels.  
In fact, using the ratio of after-tax wages to inflation as a proxy for “real wages,” we can see they largely declined throughout 
2022 and then again since mid-2023, according to Bank of America internal and BLS inflation data (Exhibit 11). Additionally, the 
drop in real wages is largely mirrored by a decline in the University of Michigan’s Index of Consumer Sentiment. This suggests 
that people have been feeling worse about their finances and the broader economy as their purchasing power has declined. 
Consumers are spending more than they did last year (read more in Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers), but sentiment 
likely reflects the sacrifices consumers have made in order to keep spending.  
Looking ahead, in our view, the outlook for affordability is relatively encouraging: wage growth remains firm and has picked up 
for lower-income houses. That said, there are risks. BofA Global Research notes the potential for gasoline and grocery prices to 
increase. And higher mortgage rates make it difficult for those looking to make the jump from renting to homeownership.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Grocery, as discussed in this analysis, includes spending at grocery stores, warehouse clubs and dollar/discount stores.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

The scenario, forecasts and nowcasts of the Economic Research
